# Development log


What this fork has landed, and what was decided while landing it. Newest first.

This is the development half of the board. The other half — `1 · How the pieces fit`,
`2 · The blocks on the canvas`, `3 · Try it` — is the **Project structure** map: what the tool
is made of, right now, undated. This one is the record of how it got that way, and every row
carries the date it landed on.

It replaces `delivered.md` and `open-work.md`, which said the same thing about a smaller
repository and then stopped. `delivered.md` still opened with "Issues #1–#11, all closed" when
`main` was past #83; `open-work.md` still called #20 and #21 the open work. A document that has
to be rewritten to stay true gets rewritten once and then never again — a log that is only ever
appended to does not have that failure mode. What is still *ahead* is not here: that is
[whats-next.md](whats-next.md), and it is forward-looking on purpose.

## Adding an entry

One row per merged pull request, at the top of the table, before the pull request is merged:

| Date | Issue | PR | What was decided |
|---|---|---|---|

It reports `narrowed: false` with a reason rather than pretending, and position 15 refuses — a chat that silently has write access is worse than no chat. A `codex-cli` invocation answers `true` and hands back `--sandbox read-only`, a mode with no network in it, rather than doing nothing quietly. **The check asserts the realised argv**, never `AGENT_PERMISSIONS`: 14 cases red against a stub returning its input unchanged, every exclusion among them, and section 1 is a control that the unnarrowed grant really did reach those writes. `implementFullAccess` is untouched and cannot reach a chat — the built issue-role argv is byte-identical with the setting on and off. |

The liveness state travels **beside** `error` and never inside it, which is what keeps a laptop in a bag from making implement runs refuse. **And a board-scoped route naming an id in the reserved `peer.` namespace is refused 421 with a sentence**, in one middleware rather than at thirty call sites. #565 landed its forwarder mid-run and the two are composed rather than one picked: the proxy runs first and a board this machine still holds a credential for is answered by its owner, while the refusal covers what that seam deliberately lets fall through — a forgotten peer, the row a peer with no projects wears, a near miss in the namespace. Two of that milestone's checks read the fall-through as *200 with an empty store*; they accept the refusal too now, because a request that never reaches a handler is the stronger evidence of the same property. `PUT /api/workspaces/order` takes a `foreign` predicate so an order spanning machines is written for the half this registry owns and answered with both. |

Written temp-then-rename, because half a document read at the next start loses every record rather than the one being written. a `kind` outside the closed set is refused with a fault of its own, because TypeScript catches that at a compiled call site and nothing catches it at an HTTP one, and a record is deliberately **not** re-validated on the way in from the file — re-reading against today's rules would empty somebody's column the day a limit is tightened. **The chat is not register-validated and says so in the code**: a stack trace and 360 characters of stderr go into a turn intact, and the check asserts both halves in one place by storing that text as a turn and watching the same text refused as a `what`. **The key is the dedupe** — `<workspaceId>:<kind>[:<discriminator>]`, composed rather than typed, so the twenty-second poll, the issue panel, the queue's dependency reads and every implement start compose the same string without having heard of one another, and the second, third and fourth of them move `lastSeenAt` and **nothing else**: not the fields, because the first producer wrote the card, and not the state, because a settled key is left settled and re-opening is #545's rule to write once it has real callers. `scripts/check-founder-store.mjs` (fast, 2.7 s, no server and no browser) landed first against a module-level `Map` — **20 cases red**, the restart, corruption, null-directory and atomicity sections plus that copy case, with sections 1 and 3 to 7 green on purpose because none of them needs a disk. Two of its sections needed a seam a map has no way to fake: the `null` from `boardStateDir()` is unreachable through the environment, since `registryPath()` falls back to a path in the per-user state directory, so it is reached by a **`module.register()` load hook in a child process**; and the file is parsed by a **second process** on every tick, because the writes are synchronous and a reader in the same process could not interleave with them if it tried. The retry is now 240 ms wide, measured rather than guessed, and the check gained the case that would have caught it — two hundred records written, two hundred read back off the disk by a fresh instance. It spawned a private child on three pipes and read what it had written once it exited — `stdout += chunk`, one read on close — so a board could say a run was `running` and nothing else; the terminal was built beside it and nothing ever joined the two, which `docs/terminal.md` recorded three times and #51 deferred on purpose. **`runAgent` takes a `host`, and null is the important half.** Given one the run happens in a session opened in its worktree and owned by the issue, and the tab appears with nobody clicking for it; given none — no terminal on this board, no PTY, all eight tabs taken, a host that threw — it falls through to the private child it always used. The constructor therefore ignores the binding whenever `input` is set rather than leaving the trap for a caller, and `mode` says `pipe` so no block claims otherwise. That settles the issue's blocking open question the other way round from how it was posed: because the process inside the tab **is** the process that ran before, in the same checkout, reading the same prompt, there is no repainting screen to scrape — one `agentOutcome` decides `done` with a pull request and `failed` with an error for a watched run and an unwatched one alike. The price is a tab that is read-only in substance, and `write()` swallows a keystroke rather than echoing one that would read as though the agent had heard it. Two more answered: #122 had already landed as #139, so the marker question was moot; and the tab **does not come to the front**, because four unattended runs at the default concurrency, plus a queue that starts them with nobody watching, would take the view away from a shell somebody is typing in. `POST /api/terminal` gains an optional `command` and `cwd` so the server's own entrance is one a person can also reach — a facility only the server can use is one nothing can check by hand — with all three guards untouched, since a shell is already a thing you type commands and `cd` into. `check-implement-terminal.mjs` went 14 cases red against the old code and caught something on the way: a board running on this machine exports `EXCALIDRAW_TERMINAL=1`, so the case about a board with **no** terminal was quietly being run against one that had it. Structure map: the Terminal card says a run opens one in its worktree. Left for its own issue and written into `whats-next.md`: the **issue** agent has the same gap and the seam already exists — `runIssueAgent` simply does not pass a `host`. |

- **Date** — ISO 8601, the day it lands.
- **Issue** — the issue the branch was cut for, or `—` for the handful of early changes that
  went straight to a pull request.
- **PR** — the pull request. It is written before the merge, so the log runs one entry ahead of
  `git log`; `scripts/check-board-map.mjs` only fails the other way round, on a merge with no
  entry.
- **What was decided** — the decision, not the diff. `git log` already has the diff.

A change that also moves the architecture — a file, a route, a block kind or a feature added or
removed — updates the **Project structure** map on the board as well. `CLAUDE.md` is where that
rule lives.

## Entries

| Date | Issue | PR | What was decided |

Of the issue's two candidate fixes the general one was taken: the refusal is remembered and honoured when the counter reaches zero, through the one function every release goes through. Flushing before `POST /api/issue-block/:id` was refused as hiding a general loss behind one route. What remains open is the debounce itself — a click inside 1.2 s of the last keystroke still races the write, which is a window, not a change lost for good. |

The block is not locked and will not be: `locked` is the only thing Excalidraw's eraser respects, and it is also what gives the block its selection, its drag and its corner resize — the interface, not a detail. So the erase is undone rather than refused, and only while the shell is alive; once it has exited the block is a notice, and a notice the reader clears stays cleared. It comes back where it was rather than re-anchored to the right of the board, because a restore that also moved it would answer an accidental erase with a second one. The key stops standing down when no session is open — it POSTs one, which was inert in exactly the two cases a reader reaches for it — and it now says so on the block, where the shell's exit is already drawn. Found while building it, and the reason the restore looked like it un-erased the whole canvas: `convertToExcalidrawElements` rebuilds an element from a skeleton and has no `isDeleted` to rebuild from, so `ensureTerminalBlock` handing it the tombstones was resurrecting everything the eraser had just taken. It reads the live scene now. Left out on purpose: nothing in the frontend calls `DELETE /api/terminal`, and a way to close a session is its own decision. |

The header goes back to one number, counting everything the column holds — drafts, cards, and the cards the cap left out — rather than the mirrored cards alone, which is why a column of drafts read `Todo (0)`. Adding `My Notes` to the project stays a maintainer's step: `updateProjectV2Field` rewrites the whole option list from an input carrying no option ids, so automating it would clear every item's Status. *(Entry written after the fact, on the #93 branch: this merge landed without one, which is the direction `scripts/check-board-map.mjs` fails on.)* |

The old order — connect on `default`, then let `GET /api/workspaces` *switch* — made every refresh pay for a second connection and a blank canvas, which is how it arrived as a bug report: a blank canvas reads as data loss. Which board comes from `?workspace=` first and `localStorage` second, both answers to the issue's open question rather than one: a board gets a URL so two can be open side by side, and a plain refresh still returns where the reader was. Switching now keeps the previous board on screen and holds autosync off by name, because autosync was what the blanking was really protecting against. The pill gets a third state — a socket that has never been up is *Connecting*, and calling that Disconnected is the complaint itself — with immediate-then-backoff retries and a `ping`/`pong` heartbeat, since a browser cannot send a protocol ping and a half-open socket otherwise reads Connected until TCP gives up. `GET /api/files` is scoped by reference rather than by ownership: the store stays shared because a file is content-addressed and two boards may legitimately point at the same one. |

Absence used to mean deletion, so anything created through the API vanished on the next autosync. ## The pattern under the first eleven

Three of the first eleven issues were the same shape of bug: two writers to one store, with no
rule for what happens when they disagree. Clear-and-replace sync (#1), schemas that dropped
fields another path accepted (#2), and a global `Map` shared by every board (#8). The fix each
time was to make the rule explicit rather than to make one writer back off.

That is still the rule the newer half of this log follows. The mirror and the terminal are the
same problem read the other way: they have exactly one writer, so they are marked *derived* and
never stored at all.

## The habit that keeps costing

Every behaviour change here ships with a `scripts/check-*.mjs`, run against the old code first.
The ordering is not ceremony. Three defects in the UI layer — a panel that never opened, a race
in tab startup, a click landing on the label instead of the box — compiled, type-checked, and
did none of what they claimed. Nothing short of driving a real browser would have caught any of
them, which is why the browser checks exist and why `CLAUDE.md` says compiling is not working.
